An entry for a function that finds a method by asserting to an optional interface waits for dynamic types. The guide says to write `bw.Write([]byte(s))`.
- **A local `bufio.NewWriter` or `NewReader`** that is written through: footprints, or dynamic types (see Shipped wrappers of bufio above).
- **A vouch that is conditional on the kept value's own errors**: `Err` vouching for its result whenever the errors of the reader it keeps are vouched for. It needs a way for an entry to say that a result is vouched for exactly when what a parameter holds is (an origin that follows a kept value), and the kept reader's errors being of known origin is decided per call, not per type, so the vouch would have to travel with the Scanner from `NewScanner` to `Err`, which needs a ghost of the value the Scanner keeps.
- **Code kept and run on another goroutine, by a timer, or by a registry**: `Register` with a `Fire` that is handed nothing, `http.HandleFunc`, `time.AfterFunc`, `runtime.SetFinalizer`, and a constructor that starts a goroutine over what it keeps. No destination of `keeps` can describe them, so they have no true entry and verified code cannot hand them a value. They wait for goroutines (item 11).
- **`context.WithCancel` and its relatives.** `WithCancel(parent)` keeps `parent` in both results, so `keeps: {parent: [result1, result2]}` looks right, and is false. For a parent of a type outside the standard library it starts a goroutine that waits on `parent.Done()`. For a parent with an `AfterFunc` method it registers `child.cancel` with the parent, which runs it, and through `removeChild` the parent's `Value` and `Done`, on whatever goroutine cancels the parent, at a time no call of the caller decides. What is missing is a claim for kept code run on another goroutine, which needs goroutines, and for a `Context` that verified code could make, channels as values for `Done`. Verified code cannot make a `Context` today (`Done` has no contract), so no code of the caller could be run that way, but the claim would still be false, so it is not stretched to fit.
- **A wrapper that changes a state of what it keeps**, such as `http.MaxBytesReader`, whose `Close` closes `r`. The later call's `modifies` cannot name the kept value, which is not its parameter and lies in an unexported field, so its frame claim would be false; and its claim to call `Close` only when `~closed` holds breaks once the caller closes the inner value itself. It needs footprints (item 1).
- **JSON** is built as F29 with claims of its own, `encodes` and `decodes` (see JSON). An encoder or a decoder held in a variable waits for following a kept writer into a local, as `bufio.NewWriter` over a `ResponseWriter` does (Footprints, Not built, from F28).

### Package-level variables

Built on 2026-11-20 (F14, part 1 of three, decided by the orchestrator): package-level variables that nothing changes, of every data type the verifier models, read in bodies and named in the contracts of verified functions and in annotations in bodies, with what their initialisers give, and contents that are only read (`globals.go`). `var limits = map[string]int{"free": 10}` and `limits["free"] = 10`, `var table = []int{1, 10, 100}` read in a loop that writes another slice, and `var digits = "0123..."` verify.

Decisions:

- **One pass decides what changes a variable, for every variable** (`findChanges`, replacing the change detection of `findSentinels`). It walks every use that `types.Info.Uses` records in every file of the package, code that is not verified, initialisers, `init`, literals, `defer`, `go`, and generic code included, and decides each by its parent expressions (`useOf`). A change: an assignment of the variable or of a part reached through struct fields and array elements, `++`, a `range` target, `&`, slicing an array value, or a method with a pointer receiver selected on such a part (call, method value, `defer`, `go`, promoted). A `//go:linkname` directive changes its local name, and publishes a write of its target whatever it names, so its direction is never read (F2 of the design review). A reference from assembly (`pass.OtherFiles`, `·` and `∕` rewritten in each symbol, `·name` for the package itself) changes the variable, read or write. Changes of variables of other packages are published as writes, as for error variables, and the reliance on a value is published and met in every package built with both. This closed a hole that error variables had: a linkname pull from another package changed a sentinel that a proof relied on.
- **A fixed variable is a value, not memory.** Its leaves are constants `var <path>.<name><leafpath>`, as a sentinel's, the same in every function and package, exempt from the renaming of sub-verifiers. Facts are noted once per state: the type facts against the entry bound, so what it refers to existed on entry, and what the initialiser gives (`knownOf`): zero without an initialiser, a constant, a struct literal part by part, a made slice, map, or pointer (not nil, the length and capacity of a literal or of `make` with constants, apart from other made allocations with a size, F4), or the value of another fixed variable. Anything else gives nothing, and so does `//go:embed` (found by position, as the compiler does, F8). Only data types: no function, interface, error, channel, or `unsafe.Pointer` at any depth, pointees and unexported fields of structs of other packages included (F6, F16). Exempting unexported fields was tried and was unsound: verified code cannot read them, but it can hand the value to a trusted function that runs the code they hold. Two initialisers arranged `var logger = log.New(tally, "", 0)` with a writer that writes what `var tally = &Tally{}` points to, and `logger.Printf`, under its entry with `runs: [l]`, was taken to write nothing a caller can see (`globalsclient.Quiet`). So `*os.File`, `*log.Logger`, and `*http.Client` cannot be read. The other rules that decide whether a value can carry code (`holdsCode` for what a trusted function or a call through a value may be handed, `holdsCallable` for stratification, `foreignCode`) count an `unsafe.Pointer` too since then, so that one question has one answer (decided by the orchestrator); no program of the corpus changed. `holdsContractedValue` does not, since verified code cannot reach a value through one.
- **Contents that are only read are a constant** (F1, F3, F10, F11 of the review). When the initialiser itself made the memory (a slice or map literal, `&T{...}`, `make`, `new`, `[]byte` or `[]rune` of a constant), and every use in the build only reads a flat value out of the variable, nothing else refers to that memory and nothing writes it. `table()` returns, for a read at the variable's own reference, a constant `var <K> in <region>`, skipping every write, layer, and join, and a literal gives the elements, the keys and values with a quantified fact that no other key is present, or the fields (`noteContents`, at most 1024 entries, and only for the initialiser itself). A read is a path of fields and elements ending in a flat value, `len`, `cap`, a comparison with nil, a two-result index, or a `range` that copies out only flat values; anything else is an escape, so a form nobody thought of is one. An escape of an exported variable in another package is published under `K[]` and met like a write. A rule that also demanded flat element types was dropped, since no test could tell it from the list of reads.
- **Settledness, as for errors.** When `hiddenInitCode` finds code that Go's order of initialisation does not see, a variable with an initialiser cannot be read (decided by the orchestrator, O1); a variable without one is zero before any code runs and stays readable.
- **A `Requires` about package-level variables alone must follow from their declarations** (F5). No caller chooses such a value, and an importer may not be able to name it, so a top-level conjunct that reads only package-level variables is proved from what their declarations give, or reported at the clause. A conjunct that relates a parameter to a variable is a condition on callers as usual. Struct invariants, contracts of function types and interface methods, and trusted contracts may not name non-error variables yet.
- **Fixed variables are inputs of the always-fails check** (decided by the orchestrator, O2). A sample gives a value to each variable the formulas mention, and the facts known of every input are applied to it (`witness.fixedOnly`); applying the whole witness there changed the cost of an unrelated function, so only these constants are replaced.
- **Writes are refused with a message that says why.** Assigning a variable, writing what it refers to, `delete`, a `Modifies` entry that names one, and a write by a call or an `append` into memory at a fixed variable's reference say that a verified function cannot do this, or that a clause cannot name it, yet (F14).
- **No trust-file entries for variables** (decided by the orchestrator, O5): what can be known is read from source, as for errors.
- **A failed clause names the use that lets the contents out** (F15, at the orchestrator's request). When a function reads a variable whose initialiser made what it refers to, and a use in the build lets a reference to that out, so that the contents are ordinary memory, every obligation that fails there (`check`: contract clauses, and the built-in checks of indexes, overflow, division, and nil, F20 of the review) ends in `; the contents of sizes are not known to stay as initialised: the use at globals.go:49 lets a reference to them out`, with the package for a use in another package (`noteLostContents`, from `escapes` and the `K[]` writes of the build). It is stated as a fact, not as the cause: the obligation may fail for another reason. `globalsleakclient` holds the case of another package.
- **Assumed**: no code outside the build changes a variable a proof relies on or lets out a reference to read-only contents, and nothing does through `unsafe`, `reflect`, cgo, or a `.syso` object; the build sets no variable a proof relies on with `-ldflags -X` (F9).

What the change found in the existing corpus: nothing in the corpus or the guide changed message, and two programs that read an `int` nothing assigned (`guide.Counted`, `rejected.ReadsGlobal`) verified and now assign it, to keep the message for a changed variable.

Built as F31 (2026-11-23): parts 2 and 3, item 3 of the list at the top. The orchestrator decided the scope and D1 to D7, and the reviewer agreed the design (F1 to F13 of its review). The parts are built in this order:

1. assigned variables of data types;
2. invariants on variables;
3. variables of interface and function types.

Part 4 (struct variables whose address is taken, `held(mu)` on a variable, initialisers that call functions, the initialiser-only mark of D4, and `var logger = log.New(...)`) is in this feature only if the first three come to no more than about fourteen commits. Otherwise it is the next feature.

The design, as decided before the code:

- **Four kinds of variable, decided for the whole build** by `findChanges`, as now.
  - **Fixed**: nothing changes it. Part 1's model is unchanged.
  - **Assigned, its address never taken (kind 2)**: its storage lies in regions of its own, one per leaf, keyed `var <key><leafpath>`, at a constant reference that existed on entry.
  - **A struct variable whose address is taken (kind 3, part 4)**: an allocation of its own in its struct's regions, as a boxed local has.
  - **Init-only (kind 4)**: every change is an assignment in the body of a `func init` of its own package, outside every function literal, its address is never taken, and no `go` statement names it (F4 of the review). Importers see it as fixed with an unknown value, and its own package sees it as kind 2. As built, kind 4 was folded into kind 2 and has no code of its own: see "Kind 4 is not a kind of its own" under step 3 of part 1.
- **What is known on entry** of an assigned variable: its type facts against the entry bound, the invariants of its group, and the `Requires`. Its initialiser gives nothing.
- **A `Requires` that reads an unexported package-level variable** is refused in a function that code of another package can call: an exported function, a method with an exported name, or one made into a value (F12, decided by the orchestrator). No caller there can name the condition, establish it, or see it fail. The remedy is a test in the body or an invariant. Elsewhere such a clause is a condition on callers.
- **A changed variable of type `error` stays `cannot verify`** (F8, a narrowing; see Not built).
- **A variable on either side of `//go:linkname`, or named from assembly, stays `cannot verify`** wherever it is read or written, in every kind (F3). Its storage is another symbol's, which code writes under the other name.

The invariants the design rests on:

- **V1, storage.** The storage of a kind-2 variable is reached only by naming it. No pointer refers to it, so nothing aliases it, and no footprint row holds a cell of it.
  - Verified code cannot make a row hold it. A conversion needs an address, and a method check refuses a write outside the receiver's row.
  - Code that is not verified is covered by the assumption, stated in the guide, that a value from code that is not verified writes only what its type's contract lists.
  - That the address is never taken is a reliance that the package publishes and every package built with it meets (F2). `&lib.Cfg` in an importer is reported there, verified or not.
- **V2, frames.** Every write of a variable's storage, or of memory it refers to that existed on entry, is covered by the frame of the function that makes it: by the variable's name, by a package state (D1), or by a parameter path that reaches the memory. The frame check is transitive, so a call whose callee's frame does not cover a variable leaves it unchanged.
  - What a call through a value may change, by the call graph's reach into the package's own code, is a target of the call for the frame check, not only forgotten (F7, decided by the orchestrator). The caller lists it or is refused.
- **V3, group invariants.** The invariant of a group holds whenever no function of the package that touches the group is running on the goroutine. It rests on four rules (part 2):
  - initialisation establishes it;
  - every writer is a verified function of the package and restores it on return;
  - it is proved before every call that may reach a function that touches the group;
  - what it reads is reached only through the group's variables.
- **V4, rows and origins.** What a variable refers to lies outside every footprint row only while the variable holds the only reference to it. As built (`packageOrigin`, F1, F21 of the review of step 3), a read of a package-level variable that refers to memory is outside only when two reliances hold, each published and met by every package built with the relier:
  - its initialiser made the memory, and no use in the build lets a reference to it out (part 1's read-only contents, `contentsKey`);
  - nothing in the build writes the variable (its value key).

  Otherwise the read is of unknown origin. That covers every assigned variable, so the design's two other reliances are not built and not needed now: that every store into the variable stores an outside value, and that its memory is never converted. Every use that brings a memory-holding read of a variable to the origin analysis, by handing it to a call or storing it, is itself an escape. So the outside case is reached by no program today. A later change that stops counting some use as an escape, or lets an assigned variable read as outside, owes those two reliances, published and met as the others are. Before F31 a read of any package-level variable counted as outside, which was unsound (F1 of the review; `globals/origins.go`).
- **V5, initialisation.** Code of an importer of P runs only after P's initialisation is complete. Go initialises a package after what it imports, and code that runs before P's init cannot name an importer of P. Hidden init code and linkname are refused, as part 1 refuses them. Kind 4 was folded into kind 2, so V5 is used now only to say that the value an initialiser or `init` gives a variable is irrelevant to an importer: an importer knows only what an assigned variable's type and the contracts it calls say of it.

The state of a package (D1, decided by the orchestrator):

- **What it covers.** A `Modifies` entry may name the state of a package other than the function's own. That state is every variable the package declares, what those variables refer to, and the states that its functions list, transitively. The last part is published as a fact: `covers(P) = {P} ∪ covers(Q)` for every package Q whose state or exported variable a contract or trust entry of P lists, computed in import order.
- **Inside its own package** a function names its variables one by one. An exported variable of another package may be named one by one too.
- **Two spellings.**
  1. The package name as the package imports it: first among the imports of the contract's file, then among those of the package's other files.
  2. The import path as a string, `@Modifies "example.com/stats"`.

  Either way the package must be a dependency of the contract's package at some depth, or the entry is refused (F9). Then its covers are always known where the entry is read.
- **Clashes.** A parameter, result, or variable of that name shadows the package name, as in Go. Two packages of one name imported in different files, with neither in the contract's file, are refused at the entry, and the message lists both paths.
- **The property that makes it usable:** a caller can always cover a write by naming a package it imports, or by the path of a package it depends on (F16 of the review of step 1). Its argument: a write the caller must cover comes from a call. For a static call of an imported package's function, that function's frame lists the state, and the imported package's covers include it. For a call through a value of a type of a package the caller does not import, the path names the type's package's state, which is a dependency through the value's origin.
- **The message for a missing entry** names the variable and the shortest entry the caller's file can write:
  1. the variable's package, if the file imports it;
  2. otherwise the imported package of the callee whose state covers it;
  3. otherwise the path.

  It is guarded by the four-package chain `stats` ← `handler` ← `app` ← `main`, in both directions.

Calls through values, decided by the orchestrator:

- **The package's own code.** What a call through a value or an interface value may reach of the package is computed from the call graph. What that code may change is forgotten and must be covered by the caller's frame. The invariant of a group is proved before such a call when what it reaches touches the group.
- **Code of other packages.** Only for code of another package is it assumed that a value from code that is not verified changes no package-level variable its type's contract does not list, also not by calling a function that does. The guide states this with the example of a plugin whose `Write` calls a package's `Hit`.
- **The remedy (part 3).** A contract of a function type or of an interface method may list variables and package states under `Modifies`. `madeAs` checks values that verified code makes against it.
- **Code of the package that another package keeps** (F6, F13, decided by the orchestrator). Storing code of the package into a variable of another package, or handing it to another package's parameter that keeps it, is `cannot verify` until item 11, with D3's message. A verified callee whose summary stores the parameter into a package-level variable counts as keeping it. A call of a function of another package counts as reaching every value of the package that the package lets out into memory it did not make (F13, part 2).
- **Trusted entries** may list variables and package states under `modifies`. An entry is a defect where it is used when its function's body, or a static callee of it in its package or in another package (by the callee's published frame), writes a variable that verified code relies on and the entry does not list (F10).

Concurrency (D5): a proof covers one goroutine. A proof of a function that changes a variable says what holds if no other goroutine reads or writes the variable while it runs. It says nothing of data races. A group invariant holds where at most one goroutine at a time runs functions of the package that touch the group. A lock discipline waits for item 11, and `held(mu)` may be required and proved as any state.

Built in step 2 of part 1 (`assigned.go`): assigned variables of the package's own data types.

- **Kind 2 in the function.** A variable of the package that the build changes only by assignment, outside every directive and assembly reference, is read and written as memory: the cells of its type, keyed `var <key><leafpath>`, at `varref <key>`, which is not nil and lies below the entry bound. A read is a recorded read, so the order rule sees it. A store is a write that `checkWriteOr` checks against the frame. A loop forgets what its body assigns by region, and an in-place or deferred literal writes it in place. A value made by verified code reads it from its own memory when it runs, not from the copy of what was known where it was made.
  - **V1 in the model.** `inEpoch` returns nothing for a `var ` region, and `startEpoch` copies the regions the state holds, so a write through a footprint keeps them. A loop that writes a footprint layers no footprint target over them. `checkWriteOr` counts no footprint as covering a cell of one.
  - **Contracts.** `Modifies v`, by `variableTarget`, covers the storage of `v` and, when its type is a slice, a map, or a pointer, what that refers to, as a target beside it (`target.also`, `shape.also`, spread by `targets` and by the loop's `callSitesOf`). A path such as `v.f` or `m[k]` follows the type from the storage, by `walkTarget`, which the walk of a parameter's path became. In an `Ensures`, `v` is the value on entry and `v'` the value on return. In a body, `old(v)` is the value on entry (`locateAssigned`).
  - **A fixed variable's contents.** What a fixed variable of the package refers to may be written under `Modifies v`, which covers it as a parameter of its type is covered. Such a write is an escape, so those contents are ordinary memory.
  - **The always-fails check** gives the storage of each assigned variable that the formulas read a sample value.
  - **F12.** `checkHiddenRequires` refuses a top-level conjunct of a `Requires` that reads an unexported assigned variable, in a function that is exported, is a method with an exported name, or is named anywhere other than as the function of a call (`callableElsewhere`).
  - **Still refused**, each with a message that says why: a variable whose address is taken (part 4), a variable named by a directive or from assembly (F3), a changed error variable (F8), a variable of a type that can hold code (part 3), and a variable of another package that is changed (the next step).
- **Programs.** `globals/assigned.go` holds both directions:
  - verified: a counter that saturates, a caller that lists it, a loop, a struct's fields, deferred and in-place literals, a table filled under `Modifies`, a map made and written, a write through `w` beside the counter, and a sample of the always-fails check;
  - refused: a write not listed, a caller that does not list what its callee changes, a value read twice around a call that changes it, a loop's write, the order rule, `old`, a value's stale read, and a hidden `Requires` in an exported function and in one made into a value.

  `guide/globals.go`, `globals/defects.go`, `rejected.ReadsGlobal`, and `guide.Counted` changed with the messages: what was refused as "not supported yet" now verifies or is refused by the frame.
- **Wrong versions caught:**
  - var regions put into epochs (`ReadAroundAnswer`, in the completeness direction);
  - the regions not carried into an epoch (`WriteThenAnswer`);
  - a loop that does not forget an assignment (`LoopKeeps`);
  - a call that does not forget its var targets (`HitTwice`);
  - the frame check skipped for var regions (the needed-entry reports);
  - no sample (`Busy`);
  - the hidden `Requires` check off (`Hidden`, `valued`), and a function made into a value not counted (`valued`).

  Not observable: the fact that the reference lies below the entry bound. Without it no write is shown to be to new memory either.
- **Cost:** no row of the base moved but the programs above, which now run where they were refused at once.

Repairs after the review of step 2 (F17 to F19), and step 3 of part 1 (`pkgstate.go`): other packages.

- **F7, built (F17 of the review).** `reachedTargets` gives a call through a function value, a call of an interface method, and a call of a trusted function the targets of what code of the package the call may run writes. That code is found by the call graph's edges of the call (only its sites for a trusted function), closed over the edges of the code reached. A verified function reached adds what its frame names and is not walked further. Any other code adds every variable of the package whose use in its body changes it, or lets a reference out of one that refers to memory, which may be a write. They are targets of the call (`target.reached`): forgotten, checked against the caller's frame with a message that says code of the package that the call may run writes them, and forgotten by a loop that makes the call (`callSites`).
  - **What it costs.** A package that exports a function that changes a variable makes every call through a value of that function's signature list it. The guide says so, with `Walk` and `WalkListed`. The guide's own counter setters became unexported or took a parameter so that `defer cancel()` in `Work` lists nothing.
  - **Programs:** `globalsvalues`. `Call`, `Direct`, `Twice`, `Around`, and `AroundUnlisted` are refused; `CallListed` and `AroundListed` verify.
- **F18.** The reliance that an assigned variable's address is never taken, and that no directive names it, is published (`addressKey`). A package that takes `&lib.Cfg`, or names it in a directive, publishes a write that meets it, reported where it stands whether the function is verified or not (`assignedclient`).
- **F19.** When nothing a function's `Modifies` lists is shown to cover a write, and a listed variable that refers to memory was assigned on the path, the message says that the variable may have been assigned since, by a statement or by a call that lists it, and that a call that keeps it can say `m' = m`. The guide shows `addTwo` and `addTwoKept`. A target of a variable is spelled by its name, not as "what it calls" its name.
- **Variables of other packages.** A variable of another package is kind 2 in this build when its package publishes it as assigned (`fixedFact.Assigned`), or when its package leaves it alone and this package only assigns it, and when this build takes no address of it and no directive names it. It is read and written as an assigned variable of the package is. A contract names an exported one by its path, as in `Modifies lib.Exported`, and an unexported one only through the state of its package. That is refused with the remedy: `stats.requests is not exported; list the state of package stats, as // @Modifies stats`. A contract of another package names its variables in its own spelling, which the contract fact maps to keys (`modifiesPath`).
- **Kind 4 is not a kind of its own.** A variable that only its package's `init` assigns, as `os.Args`, is assigned in its package's build and so is kind 2 for importers: it is read as memory, nothing in the build writes it after initialisation, and a call forgets it only if the call's frame covers it. Kind 4 would have read it as a fixed constant, which no program needs, and it gave nothing that kind 2 does not. F4's literal in `init` that runs later is covered the same way. So the design's kind 4 was folded into kind 2 and not built (V5 still holds and is what makes the initialiser's value irrelevant).
- **D1, built.** A `Modifies` entry that is an import path as a string, or a name that the contract's file, or one of its package's other files, imports a package as, names the state of that package (`modifiesPackage`).
  - **Refused:** a package that is not a dependency (F9), the function's own package, and a name that two files import as different packages with neither in the contract's file, with the paths listed (`chainclash`).
  - **What it covers.** `coversFact` publishes `covers(P)`. `stateTarget` gives a target for every variable of every covered package that verified code can write: the storage of an assigned one and what it refers to, and what a fixed one refers to.
  - **The message for a missing entry** at a call (`stateRemedy`) names the variable as `the state of package P (P.v)` and the shortest entry the file can write: P, or the imported package of the callee whose state covers it, or the path.
  - **Programs:** `chainstats` ← `chainhandler` ← `chainapp` ← `chainreport`, in both directions: a caller three calls up covers the write by naming the package it imports, a third package forgets the counter across a call that lists the state, and keeps it across one that does not.

Repairs after the review of step 3 (F20 to F23), and step 4 of part 1: trusted entries.

- **F20.** What a call through a value may reach no longer stops at the package's own variables.
  - **A verified node reached** adds every variable and package state that its frame names, of any package.
  - **Code that is not verified** adds what each function of another package that it calls by name writes, as that package publishes it in `writesFact`. That fact holds, for every function with a body in every package of the build, the variables its body assigns (`Keys`, with where) and those that refer to memory and that a use lets a reference out of (`Through`), each closed over the calls it makes by name, into other packages by their facts. A function with no fact writes nothing that its body shows: one written in assembly can only change a variable that assembly names, which is refused.
  - **What no proof reads.** A variable of another package counts only when a proof can read it (`readable` in `reachedWrites`): it is exported, or its package has a function with annotations (`coversFact.Verified`). An unexported variable of a package with none, such as what `errors.New` and `fmt.Errorf` write of package `runtime`, is read by no proof.
  - **A target that cannot be built** makes the call `cannot verify`, where it was dropped before.
  - **A state that covers no variable** verified code can write gives no target.
  - **Programs:** `reachclient`. `Around` is refused, and `AroundListed`, `Fires`, and `FiresTock` forget the count. Wrong versions caught: the own-package filter on what a callee writes (`Around`), and on what a verified node's frame names (`FiresTock`).
- **F21.** V4 now says what is built and why the other two reliances are not needed.
- **F22.** The guide has a section on variables of other packages (`guide/others.go`, `guidestats`, `guidehandler`), and no longer says that a changed variable of another package cannot be read.
- **F23.** An entry that starts with the name of a package that the contract's package depends on but no file of it imports is refused with what to write instead: a package whose state covers it, or the import path.
- **Trusted entries.** A trusted entry may list, under `modifies`, a variable of its function's package by name, one of another package as `pkg.V` (resolved as an error variable of another package is), and the state of a package by its import path in quotes. That includes its own package's state, since an entry cannot name what that package does not export.
  - **F10's check** (`checkTrustedWrites`). At a call of a trusted function, every variable that the call's `writesFact` says its body, or a function it calls by name, assigns is reported when the calling function reads it and no target of the call covers it: "the trusted contract of Tick does not list trustedwrites.Ticks, which its body assigns at lib.go:8 in bump; an entry lists what its function changes, as in modifies: [Ticks]". It reads assignments only, not what a body lets out of a variable, so that an entry is not reported for passing a table along. It is a check of what source shows, not a proof of the entry.
  - **Programs:** `trustedwrites` and `trustedwritesclient`, with entries at the end of the trust file of the tests. Wrong version caught: the check off (`Same`).
- **Facts registered.** `fixedFact`, `coversFact`, and `writesFact` are in `Analyzer.FactTypes`. `fixedFact` had been exported without being registered since part 1, which a driver that encodes facts, as `go vet -vettool` does, would refuse.
- **F24 and F25 (review of step 4).**
  - **F24.** The check of a trusted entry reports at every use, for every variable a proof can read whose storage the body or a callee assigns, or whose memory it writes through the variable's path (`pathWritten`: `Table[0] = 5`, `cfg.n++`, `delete(m, k)`). It no longer asks that the calling function read the variable. A use that only hands a reference on is not counted. Programs: `trustedwritesclient.Ticked` and `Poked`.
  - **F25.** `exportWrites` follows the calls without a static callee by the call graph's rule, computed from the package's own declarations: a call through a function value reaches every function, method value, and literal of the package used as a value of an identical signature and every exported function of it; a call of an interface method reaches every method of that name and signature of the package. Closed across packages by the facts, as before. Giving such a call what the whole package writes was tried first: through `runtime`, it made every shipped entry of `fmt` write `runtime.MemProfileRate`. Programs: `dynlib`, `dynhook`, and `dyniface`. Wrong versions caught: calls through values not followed (`dynhook.Around` verifies), interface methods not followed (`dyniface.Around`), and writes through a path not counted (`Poked`); the closure over callees dropped was caught by the reviewer (`Same`).
  - **F27 (review of the end of part 1).** The writes a trusted function's body publishes leave out its calls through a parameter that its entry lists under `runs`. That code is the caller's, which the caller counts at the call from the call's sites. Before, the guide's trusted `count` and `Fire` were reported as writing what any exported `func(int)` of the guide writes, and the guide's setters had been made unexported to hide that. They are back. Program: `trustedwritesclient.Applies`, a true entry that is not reported.
- **The trial and the time at the end of part 1.**
  - **The trial:** 74 of 152, as before. The one diagnostic that changed is `laascmd.main`, which reads `os.Args` now and stops at `os.Args[1:]`'s bound and at `os.Exit` (item 8). `nodelist.println`, `allpaths`, and the `digraph` command wait for part 3: `stdout` and `stdin` are interface values.
  - **The time** (F28 of the review of the end of part 1), measured at the commit that ends part 1's repairs and starts part 2 (58384e4). `go test -count=1 ./vego`, cold and back to back under a load of 3 to 6, alternating: 147 s, 144 s at the base, 181 s, 139 s at the base, and 148 s. The 181 s run, under the highest load, is not counted, which leaves 4 to 9 s over the base. The earlier pair, 161 s and 163 s, was measured at b7d45ee, before `exportWrites` was rewritten. Part 1 and the first step of part 2, with their 15 new corpus packages, have used most of the 10 s that the feature may add, so part 3 must add little.

Built in step 1 of part 2 (`varinv.go`): invariants of package-level variables.

- **The declaration.** An `Invariant` in the doc comment of a `var` declaration holds of the variables of that declaration: the spec, or the parenthesised block, which is the group (`varDocumented`, `addVarInvariant`).
- **Refused at the declaration** (`checkVarGroups`), each with a message that says why:
  - a variable that is exported;
  - one whose address is taken, that a directive names, or that is an error;
  - one that a function that is not verified changes, an `init` or a test without a contract included, or code outside every function (`unverifiedWriter`, D2);
  - one that refers to memory and has a use that lets a reference to it out, as returning it or handing it to a call (`groupEscape`). Reads, writes through its path, and assignments of memory made where it stands are allowed. That is narrower than the design's rule, which would allow handing it to a verified callee that keeps nothing; it is not built.
  - a group with an initialiser in a package with hidden init code.
- **Initialisation** (`checkGroupsInitialised`, run beside `checkInvariantsDeclared` by the verification and by speculative verifiers alike). Each variable of the group holds what its initialiser evaluates to, run as code by the verifier, or its zero value, and the invariant must hold of that. While it runs, a fixed variable of the group is read from storage (`initStorage`).
- **Where the invariant is assumed and proved.**
  - **Assumed** on entry of a function that names a variable of the group in its body or contract (`groupsNamed`), and after a call whose targets cover one.
  - **Proved** on return of a function whose frame covers one (`atReturn`), and before a call that may reach a function that touches the group (`callGroups`: a static callee of the package that touches it, by the call graph's closure, or what the call graph's edges of a call through a value, an interface method, or code handed to a trusted function reach), when the calling function's frame covers the group. A function whose frame covers none of the group's variables cannot break the invariant, so it needs neither the assumption on entry nor the proofs before calls. Assuming and proving them there made every function of the guide whose calls reach the guide's exported getter assume the invariant, at up to 27 times the cost.
- **Not built in step 1** (the first two built in step 2, below):
  - a frame of the invariant, which is proved again by Z3, with the same terms where nothing changed;
  - the reach rule's allowance for a verified callee that keeps nothing;
  - initialisers that call functions;
  - (built and tested since) an `init` with a contract is verified as any writer of the group: it assumes the invariant on entry, which the declarations give, and proves it on return (`varinv/init.go`).
- **Programs:**
  - `varinv`, both directions: a cache and its size, a counter, a limit used as a divisor, a broken writer, a call while broken, a write through a writer that reads the counter while broken, and an invariant the declaration does not give;
  - `varinvbad`, the refusals;
  - the guide's `markSeen` and `markUncounted`.

  The note on lost contents is not added for a variable of a group, whose contents are reached only through it.
- **Cost:** no row of the base moved.

Step 2 of part 2, with the repairs after the review of step 1 (F29 to F32), at the orchestrator's request.

- **The frame serves the invariant of a group.** A group carries an `invariantSet` with no value (`invariantSet.group`), whose environment reads the group's variables in the memory given (`invariantEnv`). An assumption on entry, or after a call whose frame covers the group, is a K entry (`assumeGroup`). Each proof goes through `checkClauseFramed` with `framedStep`, as a type's invariant does, so a clause is kept where nothing it reads changed since a point where it held. A message that fails names the write that may have lost it. That needed nothing but the set without a value. Program: `varinv.reprice` (a quantified invariant over `prices` kept between two calls of `setPrice` across a write of another group). Not observable by a wrong version: without the frame Z3 proves the corpus's clauses again, at some more cost.
- **The memory of a group handed to a callee that only reads it** (`handedToReader`). An argument of a call counts as no escape when the callee keeps nothing of the parameter and writes nothing through it: a verified function whose ownership summary keeps nothing and whose `Modifies` does not name the parameter, or a trusted one whose entry neither keeps nor modifies it. A callee that writes through it is refused, since it would break an invariant it does not know of. Programs: `varinv.NamesCount` verifies; `varinv.Blank`'s group is refused.
- **F29.** An invariant may read only the variables of its group and variables that nothing in the build changes. The variables it reads are collected while its initialisation is checked (`groupNames`). Program: `varinvbad`'s `lo` and `hi`.
- **F30, F13 built.** `letOut` gives the functions, methods, and literals of the package that it stores into memory (through a pointer, a field, or an element, or into a variable of another package) or hands to a function of another package that may keep them. A call of a function of another package, a trusted function, a function value, or an interface method counts as reaching each of them for `callGroups`. Programs: `letoutq`, `letoutp`, and `letoutmain`. A verified `Setup` that installs `check` into `letoutq.Slot()` cannot be written, since what `Slot` returns is memory the function did not make and cannot list.
- **F31.** A `Modifies` path through a variable, as `entries[k]` with a parameter `k`, reads the parameter: `variableTarget` takes the arguments. Programs: `globals.putEntry` verifies, and `putOther` is refused.
- **F32.** The guide says what is built: a function that names the variables assumes the invariant. It also gives D5's condition for invariants, that at most one goroutine at a time runs functions of the package that name the group.
- **Wrong versions caught:** what the package lets out ignored (`letoutp.Bump` verifies), and the names of an invariant outside its group not checked (`lo` accepted).

Repairs after the review of step 2 of part 2 (F33 to F35).

- **F33.** For a call into another package, a trusted call, and a call through a value or an interface method, `callGroups` also counts every exported function and every method with an exported name of the package. Any package can make a value of one and keep it, as the call graph says of a call through a value. Program: `exportedp.Bump` is refused, with `exportedmain` as the unverified form. The assumption under Cycles in "What a proof assumes" now speaks of code of another package that is not verified, as the orchestrator ruled on F6. What verified code of another package runs is what the call graph and `letOut` follow.
- **F34.** The reader allowance also asks that no result of the callee can hold the parameter's memory or memory derived from it.
  - **A verified callee:** no result of its ownership summary flows from the parameter, lies inside it, or is unknown.
  - **A trusted one:** no result holds memory at all, since an unplaced result may be any argument's (F14 of ownership in source).

  Program: `readerbad` (`same` and `slices.Clip` handing `items` back) is refused at the declaration.
- **F35.** What a variable of a group refers to lies outside every footprint row. Each separation of a listed footprint takes the values on entry of the groups' variables beside the other parameters (`groupRoots` in `enterReaches`), so a footprint layer keeps it.
  - **Why it is true.** The rules of a group make that memory reached only through its variable: every use reads it, writes through its path a value that refers to no memory or to memory made where it stands (F36), assigns it memory made where it stands, or hands it to a callee that neither keeps nor returns it, and no code that is not verified writes it. So nothing could have converted it into a writer, stored it where a writer reaches, or placed it in a row.
  - **A rule this needed.** Its initialiser must make what it refers to (`knownMade`, the zero value, or nil), since memory that came from elsewhere may be held by a writer. Program: `varinvbad.alias` is refused.
  - **Programs:** `varinv.touchThenWrite` and `writeThenReset` verify, the orchestrator's case: a quantified invariant over a map kept across a write through a writer the function lists. `writeNegative` is refused.
- **F36.** A store through a group variable's path must store a value that refers to no memory or memory made where it stands (`pathStoreFresh`, `madeHere`), the shape of G and R1 for owned fields; a store of a reference to memory something else holds refuses the group with the use named, as does a range whose target in the path takes such a value. Without it the group's memory could be reached through another reference, which breaks both the invariant and F35's statement. Programs: `pathstore` (`link`, `spoil`, `across`) is refused; `varinv.relink` and `Peek` verify. Wrong version caught: any store through the path allowed (`pathstore` accepted).
- **F37.** Made where it stands holds at every depth (`madeHere`): every field, element, key, and value of a literal that holds a reference is itself nil, made where it stands, or a value that holds none; `[]byte` or `[]rune` of a constant string is made there too. It applies to a store through the path, an assignment of the variable, and the initialiser alike (the initialiser's test was `knownMade`, which looks only at the top). Programs: `madehere` (a literal that holds a parameter stored through the path, assigned, and an initialiser that holds another variable's memory) is refused; `relinkFresh` verifies. Wrong version caught: only the top checked (all three accepted at the declaration; `c` is then refused only by its initialisation check).
- **Wrong versions caught:** the exported functions left out of `callGroups` (`exportedp.Bump` verifies), the results of a verified reader not checked (`readerbad` refused only at `slices.Clip`, line 36, where it is refused at `same`, line 29), and the group roots left out of the separation (`touchThenWrite` and `writeThenReset` refused). The separation takes the roots only in a function that names a variable of the group: taking them in every function with a listed footprint changed the guide's quoted costs of `Load` and `DropThenAnswer`.

Part 3 (`valueVariable` in `assigned.go`): variables of interface types and of named function types with a contract.

- **What they are.** A variable whose own type is an interface type, or a named function type with a contract, may be read and assigned as any assigned or fixed variable. A call through it is a call of its type's contract, as through a parameter. What a test or other code that is not verified stores there is assumed to meet the contract. A value that a verified function makes is checked where it is made (`madeAs`). A variable of any other type that can hold code, as `*log.Logger` or a struct with a function field, stays refused.
- **`Modifies v` of an interface variable** covers its storage and the footprint and states of the value it holds on entry, as an entry of a parameter does (the contents target, which `variableTargetAny` now gives for an interface type too).
- **Refused:**
  - a `ResponseWriter` stored into a variable, by `refuseScoped`, since a variable is memory;
  - every store into a variable of another package whose type can hold code, named or not, contracted or not, as `flag.Usage = usage` (of type `func()`) or `lib.OnEvent = func() {...}`, with D3's message ("keeping code to run later is not supported yet"; F39, which found `flag.Usage` drawing part 1's message).
- **Not refused:** storing such code through memory another package made. The proof of an invariant counts it (F13); otherwise it is not built, and waits on item 11.
- **The initialiser** of a variable of an interface type or of a named function type with a contract is checked where it is declared, as code of the package (`checkValueInitialisers`, F41): a function literal against the type's contract, and a conversion to an interface as any conversion in verified code. Before F41 it was taken on trust, and a run-time test showed the false proof.
  - The walk of conversions now covers the initialisers of package-level variables, and go/ssa's side of it the init function go/ssa makes of them (`conversionsIn`), so `TestConversionLists` checks them too. A conversion go/ssa makes there that the walk does not list refuses the initialiser, as one in a function refuses the function (`conversions.inInit`). The test treats `(*T)(nil)`, which go/ssa folds into a constant, as a constant.
  - Variables given one call with several results, as `var a, b Pos = f()`, are refused when one of them is such a variable: no expression of its own makes the value.
  - Which initialisers: those of the variables verified code can read (`readable`): assigned, or fixed with a value a read can use (`usableFixed`). A fixed variable whose initialiser calls a function is readable, as `var made = mk()` with `mk` not verified, so its initialiser is checked too. `_` and a variable whose value is known only after code of the package has run (the `early` packages) are read by no verified code and are not checked.
  - **Reported at the reads** (F42 of the review of the fixes, ruled by the reviewer): what the check finds is captured (`pkgVerifier.capturing`) and kept (`initWhy`), not reported at the declaration. A read in verified code is refused with it: `readGlobal` for an assigned variable, `usableFixed` for a fixed one, as `cannot verify F: v cannot be relied on: its initialiser does not verify, at file:line:col: why`. An exported variable's reason is published (`initialisersFact`), and a read in an importer is refused the same way. At 62a5bcf the check reported at the declaration, so `settled`'s `var Root = context.Background()`, which nothing reads, was reported; it is clean again. Programs: `varcode/inits.go` (`useBad`, `writeBad`, `readMore`, `readPb`, `readLibRoot` refused; `useGood`, `writeGood`, `readLibStarted`, and the unread `background` clean), `varcodelib` (`Root`, `Started`, and `Ping`, which makes the package one whose initialisers are checked). Wrong versions caught: the reads not refused (`useBad` and the others accepted, and the reviewer's `hook` verifies and fails its `go test`); the fact not published (`readLibRoot` accepted).
  - Programs: `varcode/inits.go`. The initialisers of `getBad`, `wBad`, `wMulti` (`pick(two())`), and `pa, pb` do not verify, and their reads are refused; those of `getGood` and `wGood` verify.
- **Wrong versions caught:**
  - the refusal of a store of code into a variable of another package off: `setsOther` accepted;
  - the refusal limited to named types with a contract, as before F39: `setUsage` draws part 1's message;
  - the initialisers not checked: `getBad` and `wBad` accepted, and the reviewer's `hook` fails its `go test`;
  - several variables from one call not refused: `pa, pb` accepted, and a scratch module whose `usePa` calls `pa` verifies and fails its `go test`;
  - the unlisted conversion in an initialiser not refused: `wMulti` is then refused only by the rule against a call with several results as the arguments of another, so the guard stands behind that rule in the corpus.
- **Time at the end of part 3** (b38ee19): `go test -count=1 ./vego`, cold and back to back, alternating: 146 s, 147 s at the base, 141 s at the base, and 147 s. That is about 3 s over the base on average, and 7.5 s in the worst pair.
- **The remedy of the orchestrator's ruling on calls through values.** A contract of a function type, or of a method of an interface, may list assigned variables of its package under `Modifies`. A call through such a value forgets them and they are in the caller's frame; a value made in verified code is checked against them.
- **Programs:**
  - `varcode`: `println`, `setOut`, `stamp`, and `uses` verify; `printUnlisted`, `keep`, `firesUnlisted`, the literal in `writesOther`, and `setsOther` are refused; `fires` forgets what the type lists;
  - `varcodelib`;
  - the guide's `printLine`, `redirect`, and `stamped`.
- **A listed interface variable as a footprint root** (F40 of the review of part 3, ruled by the orchestrator). A declared function whose `Modifies` lists an interface variable by itself assumes that what its parameters reach lies outside the footprint of the value the variable holds on entry, beside the roots of the groups it names (`enterReaches`, the target's `fromVar`). So a write through the variable keeps what the parameters refer to, as through a listed writer parameter.
  - **After an assignment** the variable holds another value, whose footprint the separation says nothing of: the proof uses what was assigned, as for any value. `printTo` assigns `out` the writer `w` it lists and keeps the bounds of `l` by `w`'s own separation. Nothing more was needed, so nothing is refused for it.
  - **The caller's proof** (`checkVariableApart`), at each call of a verified function whose entry names the variable by itself, for each argument that holds memory:
    - the caller is a declared function whose own `Modifies` lists the variable by itself (a function literal places no footprint);
    - the variable holds the value it held when the caller was entered, proved by Z3: a callee that lists it ensures `v' = v` to let its caller call it again, as `printAll` does;
    - the argument comes from the caller's parameters or is made there (`memOrigin`), as for a writer parameter.
  - **Why the static calls are all the calls to check.** A function whose writes the separation helps writes through the variable, which is a call through an interface value, and the termination rule refuses such a function as a function value or as a method run through an interface (`top` and `topW` in a scratch module are refused so). So every verified call of one is a static call, which `checkVariableApart` sees. Nothing else observes the separation: `within(t, out)` after a write through `out` is not proved with it or without it.
  - **Not covered:** the state of a package (`Modifies pkg`) names no variable by itself, so its spread drops `fromVar`; a target that a call reaches without the callee listing it (`reached`) drops it too.
  - **Assumed** of unverified callers, in "What a proof assumes": they pass no argument that reaches memory in the footprint of the value the variable holds when the call begins.
  - **Programs:** `varcode/apart.go`. `printAll`, `printOwn`, `keeps`, and `printTo` verify; `redirectThenKeep` (assigns `out` the tally it then passes), `literalKeeps` (from a literal), and `printNames` (hands on another variable's slice) are refused. The guide's `printLines` and `printTwice`.
  - **Wrong versions caught:**
    - the separation not assumed: `printAll` and `printTo` lose the bounds of their lines;
    - the check that the variable still holds its entry value off: `redirectThenKeep` accepted; in a scratch module `keepsZero`, which claims a write through `out` leaves its tally's count alone, and `redirectZero`, which hands it the tally `out` now holds, both verify, and the `go test` that calls `redirectZero` fails;
    - the origin of the argument not checked: `printNames` accepted.
  - **The trial's `nodelist.println`** verifies once its contract lists `stdout` (see the trial below).
- **A fixed variable of an interface type** (O1 of the orchestrator's acceptance, with the reviewer's two more forms). `var errOut io.Writer = os.Stderr`, which nothing assigns, could not be written through: a write needed `Modifies errOut`, which was refused as naming a variable that refers to no memory. Now:
  - **`Modifies v`** of a fixed interface variable covers the footprint and the states of the value it holds, as for an assigned one (`variableTargetAny`). `count` (`var sink io.Writer = &counter{}`) verifies with it, and `countUnlisted` is refused with one message that says to list it. `shut` (`var closer io.Closer = os.Stdin`) passes the frame and stops only at `~closed(c)`, which nothing can know of a fixed closer.
  - **Nothing need be listed for an inert value** (`inertFixed`): the checked initialiser converts a value of a concrete type none of whose methods the interface takes writes behind its receiver (`writingImplementation`), and the interface has no states. A call that runs only those methods of the value leaves out its footprint and its states (`dropInert`): a method of the interface called on it (the receiver of the method's contract), and a function whose entry prints to it, which calls nothing of the writer but `Write`. `json.NewEncoder(w).Encode` runs `Write` through the method's contract, so it is covered too. Any other call that takes the value lists the variable. The fact is published for exported variables (`initialisersFact.Inert`): `warnLib` writes through `varcodelib.Log` with nothing listed.
  - **Why it is sound.** Such a value's footprint is empty when it is made, as at any conversion whose methods write nothing behind the receiver (the `emptied` layer of a conversion), and nothing adds to it: a row grows only by memory the value's methods create, and these list no receiver. Without states, nothing else of the value can change. A value read from the variable is the same constant at every read (`fixedValue`), so only its own calls are left out.
  - **A fixed variable of a contracted function type** is a call of the contract of its type at every call, as for an assigned one: `readTicks` verifies, `readTicksExact` (`t = 0`) is not proved.
  - **Programs:** `varcode/fixediface.go`. `warn`, `warnJSON`, `count`, `readTicks`, and `warnLib` verify; `warnListed` (nothing needs `Modifies errOut`), `countUnlisted`, `shutUnlisted`, and `shutDoor` (a state a project interface gives a value whose method lists no receiver) are refused. The guide's `warn`, `warnChecked` (`Requires errOut != nil`, which the declaration does not show), and `Now`, an exported wrapper of the clock's own type, refused as a cycle (the orchestrator's two smaller points).
  - **Wrong versions caught:**
    - inert without the states condition: `shutDoor` loses its states message and draws a contradiction of the contract of `shutter.Shut`;
    - inert without the condition on the methods: `countUnlisted` accepted, and `count`'s `Modifies sink` reported as not needed; in a scratch module, `var shared = &counter{}` and `var sink io.Writer = shared`, an `F` that claims a write through `sink` leaves `shared.n` alone verifies, and its `go test` fails;
    - `dropInert` off: `warn` and `warnLib` refused.
- **Time after F39 to F41** (2875090): `go test -count=1 ./vego/...` from `git archive` extracts, cold, alternating with the end of part 3 (e196cce) in one scope: 179 s and 162 s at e196cce, 167 s and 166 s at 2875090, so no slower than the base on average and 4 s over it in the worse pair. Every run was slower than at the end of part 3 by about the same, from the load of the machine.
- **Not built:**
  - The contract of a function type naming a package state or a variable of another package.
  - Variables of other types that hold code, and initialisers that call functions (part 4).
- **The writes fact in order.** `exportWrites` walks functions and literals in source order, so which write a message names is the same in every run. Before, map order picked it. Sorting at every call through a value cost 13 to 14 s of `go test -count=1 ./vego` (159 s and 160 s against 146 s at the base), since `exportWrites` runs on every package of the build, `runtime` included. The functions are now sorted once and indexed by signature (`sigKey`, which leaves out the names, as `types.Identical` does), and the time at the end of part 2 is 145 s against 140 s at the base, back to back under a load of 5 to 6.

Built as F32 (2026-11-24): part 4 of F31, as its record below settled it. The design was agreed with the reviewer (F1 to F9 of its review) and the orchestrator (O-a to O-d). Before any code, the invariants of F31 read as follows with what this feature adds.

- **Kind 3, a struct variable whose address is taken.** A variable of the package of a modelled struct type, not an error, named by no directive or assembly, of a type part 1's rules allow, whose address the build takes: `&v`, `&v.f` through struct fields, or a method with a pointer receiver selected on `v` or `v.f`. It may also be assigned. `&n` of a variable or field of any other type stays refused (item 7). Its storage is one allocation, a constant reference `varbox <key>` at position 0, whose cells are those of its type in the struct's own regions, states included. That allocation is not nil, lies below the entry bound, and differs from every other kind-3 variable's. Which variables are kind 3 is decided for the package before any function is walked. In a body, kind 3 has a path of its own (`boxedGlobal`), never `v.boxed`, whose readers mean "made by this function" (F2 of the design review). A package publishes its kind-3 variables. Another package covers them only through the state of the package, and cannot read or name one directly (O-d).
- **V1, storage**, is about kind 2 and is unchanged. Kind 3's cells are ordinary memory, not `var ` regions.
- **V2, frames**, holds as stated: every write of a kind-3 variable's cells is covered by its name, by a package state, or by a parameter path that reaches it, as `p.n` with `p = &cfg`. A pointer parameter of its type may point to it, and the proof weighs both cases; no separation fact is stated.
- **The closed address** (O-a, F3 of the design review). The address of a kind-3 variable is closed when every use of it in the build, in any package, verified or not, is the receiver or an argument of a call (`defer` included, `go` not) of a trusted entry that meets all of these:
  - it neither keeps nor stores the parameter;
  - every result that can hold memory is `fresh`, or placed within what an owner owns, never within the struct and never unplaced;
  - it runs nothing and calls no function, with two exceptions. It may run the converted address itself, when every method of it that the interface takes is a trusted entry that is itself such a sink, as `fmt.Fprintf(&buf, ...)` runs `(*bytes.Buffer).Write`. It may call a function it is handed, when its `calls` clause hands that function nothing that can hold memory, as `once.Do(f)` calls `f()` (F12 of the review of the answers), so that `f` cannot receive the address.

  Then no pointer outside such a call points into the variable (K2), and no interface value but the temporary converted at such a call has a row that holds a cell of it (K1). A proof that relies on it records the reliance, and a use that is not closed publishes a write under it, met like the others. Verified callees are not sinks, since one could convert its parameter and hand the interface to another verified function.
- **V4, rows and origins.** A read of `&v` or `&v.f` of a kind-3 variable is of unknown origin. A row that existed on entry, or that a call creates, holds no cell of a kind-3 variable whose address is closed (K1). Otherwise such a row may hold its cells, as any memory that existed on entry. The rows of conversions the function makes stay exact.
- **V3, group invariants.** A kind-3 variable may be in a group only when its address is closed, which gives the fourth rule. A group's escape rule also allows the address handed to a trusted callee that writes through it, keeps nothing, returns nothing of it, and runs nothing, as `Lock`, `Unlock`, and `WriteString`; the calling function covers the variable, so it is a writer that proves the invariant on return.

  A lock clause is a top-level conjunct of the invariant that reads only states of closed kind-3 variables of the group, as `~held(mu)` (F4 of the design review, ruled by the orchestrator). A lock clause is assumed on entry of, and proved on return of, only a function that touches the group and that code outside the package's verified code can enter: one another package can call (`callableElsewhere`), or one the package lets out (`letOut`). It is proved before a call only where the call may reach such a function. Every other function of the package relies on its `Requires` for the state of the mutex, as `bump` with `@Requires held(mu)`, called by `Hit` under the lock. The other clauses keep V3 as built. Why it holds:
  - `held` is about the goroutine that runs the function, and only this goroutine's calls change it;
  - every writer of the variable is verified (D2);
  - the functions in between never assume the clause;
  - a function in between that, under the lock, calls something that may reach a boundary function must prove the clause there, and fails. That is the deadlock.

  The invariant holds whenever no function of the package that touches the group is running on the goroutine.
- **V5, initialisation.** Unchanged. In addition, in a package with annotations, every initialiser is evaluated as code in `InitOrder`. A call there of a verified function whose `Requires` may not hold is reported where it stands, whether or not anything reads the variable, since a verified function's frame is proved only under its `Requires` (F1 of the design review, a repair of the base: `var x = set(-1)` broke a group's invariant, and `Get`, proved `>= 0`, returned -1).
- **Built in step 2** (`boxedglobals.go`): kind 3 in the own package. `isBoxedGlobal` decides it from what `findChanges` recorded (`changeKinds`, `addressSite`), and `fixedFact.Boxed` publishes it. A read in a body (`variable`), a write (`place` and `lvalue`, `lv.boxGlobal`), `&v` and `&v.f` (`addressOf` through `place`), the receiver of a method (`receiver`), a loop's effects (a site without a root, which may write any allocation of its regions), and annotations (`locateBoxed`, `old` in a body included) all go through the constant `varbox <key>`, noted once per path (`noteBoxed`). `Modifies v` and `Modifies v.f` are `boxedTarget`, the default case of `walkTarget` for a struct in memory, which gives the struct with its states, `base`, and what an owner held by value owns. The state of a package, what a call through a value may reach (`reachedTargets`), and F12 (by the reference) count kind 3. `memOrigin` of `&v` and `&v.f` of any package-level variable is unknown (`addressRoot`). The always-fails check gives a kind-3 variable an allocation and its cells values. A variable of another package of kind 3 is read only by a contract of its own package (`locateVariable`), so an importer uses `boxedlib.Set`'s `Ensures Cfg.N' = n`, and a write it covers is reported with the state of the package (`variableOfTarget` in `stateRemedy`). A value method on a struct that a fixed variable holds gets a copy (F9). `&n` of an `int` is refused with a message that says that only a struct variable may have its address taken.
  - Programs: `boxed` (both directions: a lock and a `defer`, `load(&cfg)`, a field written by itself, a pointer parameter that may alias, a loop, a literal, `old` in a body, a builder, a call through a value that may run an unverified writer, the always-fails sample, a value method of a fixed struct, `&n`, and `run` handing `keep` `&counted`), `boxedlib` and `boxedclient` (the state covers `Set`, a read across a call that lists the state is lost, a direct read and a name refused). `globals.Count`, refused at the base for `counted`'s method value, now runs and its contract is not proved, since `inc` may have changed `counted`.
  - Wrong versions caught: `&v` taken as outside (`run` accepted); no samples (`busy` reported as always failing); the loop's site of kind 3 left out (`loopKeeps` accepted, F13 of the review of step 2, whose `go test` gives `cfg.n = 3, proved 0`).
  - F14 of the review: a kind-3 variable of another package used as a receiver or with `&` draws O-d's message (`foreignBoxedWhy`, in `fieldPlace`), as a read does (`boxedclient.Receiver`, `Address`); before, it drew the message for assigning.
- **Built in step 3**: the closed address. `findChanges` hands every use of a variable's address to `noteAddressUse`, which records the first that is not closed (`openAt`, by `addressUseWhy` and `sinkWhy`), and publishes such a use of a kind-3 variable of another package under `closedKey`. A proof that uses the facts relies on that key (`apartFromClosed`), so a package of the build that lets the address out is reported: "the address of boxedlib.Buf is let out here, but proofs in package boxedlib rely on it being handed only to trusted functions that neither keep it nor run code that could be handed it". The facts are in `rowAt`: a row on entry, and the row of a home a call creates, hold no cell of a closed variable the function met. A sink has no result that can hold memory (`memoryFree`, F34's rule for trusted callees, which also leaves out `Bytes` placed in what the buffer owns); a generic function or a method of a generic type is no sink.
  - Programs: `closed`. `note`, `noteFormatted` (`fmt.Fprintf(&sb, ...)`), `locked` (`held(mu)` across a write through `w`), and `onced` (`~running(once')`) verify. Each twin loses what it knew across the write through `w`: `noteOpen` (`&open` returned), `lockedOpen` (a method value), `noteBytes` (`bb.Bytes()`), `keepTally` (`fmt.Fprint(&tally, ...)` runs a verified `Write`), `keepPrinted` (`fmt.Sprint(&printed)` formats it), and `lockedSpawn` (`go mu3.Lock()`). `boxedlib.NoteBuf` relies on `Buf`, and `boxedclient.Leak` is reported.
  - Wrong versions caught: the facts stated without the rule (`noteOpen` and `lockedOpen` accepted; in a scratch module `TestOpen` fails with `|open'| = 2, proved 1`); a verified callee as a sink (in the scratch module `writeTo` verifies and `TestWriteTo` fails with `|sb3'| = 1, proved 0`); results not checked (`noteBytes` accepted); formatted arguments allowed (`keepPrinted`); the methods run by the converted address not checked (`keepTally`); `go` allowed (`lockedSpawn`).
- **F15 and F16 of the review of step 3.** An entry describes what the function's own code does and says nothing of code a parameter it does not run carries, and an address converted to an interface may be asserted to any other. A sink therefore takes no other parameter that can carry code (`holdsCode`), but a function under `calls` handed only values that hold no memory (`once.Do`'s `f`), the values it formats, and the writer it prints to. An address converted at the call is closed only when every method of its type that takes the receiver's address is a trusted sink of it, whether or not the entry runs it; under `prints` only `Write` runs. An entry with defects is no sink. Before, the reviewer's program, a project entry `Show(v any, x any)` with `modifies: [x]` handed `&cfg` and a keeper, made `cfg` closed, and `go test` gave `cfg.n = 1 after note, proved 0`. A method expression, `(*T).M(&v, y)`, hands the receiver as its first argument, so the parameter is not shifted (F16); after F15 no legal entry shows the difference, since an entry cannot claim to keep a pointer to data. Programs, in `closed` with `closedq` and its entries at the end of the trust file of the tests: `keepShown` (the reviewer's form), `keepRan` (`Run(v *Shared, x any)`, route a), `keepPoked` (`Poke(v any)`, route b) refused; `noteBuilt` with `BuildExpr`'s method expression verifies. Wrong versions caught: the other parameters not checked (`keepRan` accepted), the methods of the address checked only when the entry runs it (`keepPoked` accepted).
- **F10 of the review of the design, a repair of the base: the reach of a static call.** F33 made a call into another package, a trusted call, and a call through a value reach what the package let out and every exported function and method with an exported name, so the invariant of a group was proved before such a call. A call of a local helper that makes the same call was not checked: `callGroups` for a static callee of the package followed the call graph only. In `exportedp2`, `Bump` breaks the invariant and calls `tick`, which calls `letoutq.Tick`, which runs `Check`, kept there by code that is not verified; `Bump` verified at the base, and `go test` of `exportedmain2` gave `Check, proved not to panic, panicked: runtime error: integer divide by zero`. Now a node of the call graph that calls out of the package, by name or through a value (`callNode.out`), reaches what such a call reaches (`outwardNodes`), and the closure of the call graph carries it to every static caller (`groupsTouched`). V3's third rule reads: the invariant is proved before every call that may reach, transitively, a function that touches the group, F33's reach included at every call on the way. Program: `exportedp2` refused, `exportedmain2` its unverified main; `BumpDeep`, two helpers away from `Tick`, guards the second closure (F18 of the review of F10). Wrong versions: the base, which accepts `Bump`; the second closure left out, which accepts `BumpDeep` (the reviewer's `exportedp3` then panics in its `go test`). Nothing else in the corpus or the guide changed.
- **F11 of the review of the design: `isolated` entries.** F33 makes every trusted call reach what the package let out and every exported function, since a trusted function may run code that another call set, as `log.Printf` runs the output of the standard logger and `http.Get` the transport of the default client. So no group could be broken across `mu.Unlock()`, `strings.ToUpper`, or `fmt.Fprintf(&b, ...)`. An entry may now say `isolated: true`, with what supports it in its `reason`, and a trust file may say at its top `isolated:` with a `reason`, which claims it of every function and method of its package (F19 of the review of F11: the trust report prints the claim and the file's reason): the claim that the function runs no code but what the call hands it through its arguments and receiver, and reaches no code through memory that another call set, also not through any function it calls. A call of such an entry whose arguments and receiver hold no code (`holdsCode`, so `any`, interface values, and a pointer to a struct with a function field count as code) reaches only the call graph's sites of the call, not F33's reach (`isolatedCall`, in `callGroups` and in `callNode.out`). Shipped as isolated: the files of `fmt`, `bytes`, `strings`, `strconv`, `unicode`, `unicode/utf8`, and `math`, each with a comment on what was read, and the methods of `sync.Mutex` and `sync.RWMutex` that lock and unlock. Not isolated: `log`, `net/http`, `os`, `sync.Once.Do`, and the rest. The trust report prints the claim, and the entry's fingerprint includes it. `TestIsolatedHooks` lists every hook each such package exports (an exported variable that can hold code, or a function named `Set...` or `Register...`) against a pinned list (`bytes.ErrTooLarge`, `strconv.ErrRange` and `ErrSyntax`), and runs the isolated functions with each hook set to an error whose `Error` fails the test. The reviewer's source guard (an isolated entry whose body calls through a value read from a variable an exported function writes) is not built; the pinned list stands for it. Programs: `isolatedg` with `isolatedq` and two project entries. `Upper` (refused at the base) and `Plain` verify; `Logs` (`log.Print`), `Formats` (`fmt.Sprint` of an error), and `Hands` (an isolated entry handed a box with a function field) are refused. An argument converted to an interface hands the callee the methods of its type, which fmt runs (`String`, the `Write` of the writer it prints to), so each exported one must be isolated too, by its entry or by a file-level claim, which covers the whole package; aa90b69 left that out, and `FormatsShown` (`fmt.Sprint(Shown{})`, whose `String` assumes the invariant) verified there, found by the coder. `Builds` (`fmt.Fprintf(&b, ...)` into a local builder) verifies. Wrong versions caught: the methods of a converted argument not checked (`FormatsShown` accepted); every entry taken as isolated (`Logs` accepted; in a scratch module whose test sets `log.SetOutput` to a writer of the package that divides by what the invariant keeps positive, `Bump` verifies and its `go test` panics with `integer divide by zero`).
- **Built in step 4**: groups with a struct variable whose address is closed, and lock clauses.
  - **The declaration** (`checkVarGroups`). A kind-3 variable may be in a group when its address is closed and no sink of it runs a function it is handed while it has the address (`addressRuns`, as `once.Do`). Otherwise the group is refused with the reason. An unverified function that locks it refuses the group as any unverified writer does (D2). The escape rule (`groupEscape`) lets a closed address be handed to its sinks, which keep nothing and return nothing that holds memory; the function that hands it covers the variable and restores the invariant.
  - **The model.** A group's kind-3 variable is covered by a target at its own reference (`groupsCovered`; K2 says no other pointer reaches it). Its initial value is stored at its allocation (`checkGroupsInitialised`), and what it refers to is a root of the separation of listed footprints (`groupRoots`, as a pointer to it).
  - **Lock clauses** (`lockConjunct`, `lockBoundary`, `callLockGroups`, and `touchedAtBoundary`, the closure of the call graph seeded by boundary nodes, with F10's reach of calls out). A lock clause is assumed on entry and proved on return only in a boundary function, and proved before a call only when the call may reach a boundary function that touches the group. A boundary function is one another package can call, `init`, or one the package lets out; a literal used as a value counts as one. The other conjuncts keep V3 as built (`groupHolds`, `proveGroups` with `skipConjunct`).
  - **After a call** the invariant is assumed again only of a verified callee, or of code through a value, which restore it; the lock clauses only when that callee is a boundary function or a value's. A trusted callee restores nothing: after `Lock`, the group is known by the entry's ensures and by the frame. Assuming it again there contradicts `held(m')`, which the check that a trusted contract cannot contradict what is known reports.
  - Programs: `lockgroup`. `Hit` (locks, `defer` unlocks, calls `bump` with `@Requires held(mu)`), `bump`, `Count` (`r >= 0` from the invariant), and `Note` (a `strings.Builder` in the group, written under its lock, and a write through `w` after the unlock) verify. Refused: `Unlocked` (`bump` without the lock), `HitNotify` (a call through a value under the lock, which may come back into `Hit`), `LeaveLocked` (returns holding the lock), `NoteUnder` (a write through `w` under the lock, which may reach a writer of the package that locks), and `HitTick` (the lock-clause twin of F10: `notify` calls `letoutq.Tick` under the lock). `lockgroupbad`: `Bump`, exported with `@Requires held(mu)`, and `bumpValue`, made into a value, assume `~held(mu)` and are reported as unsatisfiable; the groups of a `sync.Once`, of a mutex whose `Lock` is made into a method value, and of one an unverified function locks are refused at the declaration.
  - F20 of the review of step 4: a group with a `sync.RWMutex` crashed, since reading a kind-3 variable in an annotation noted the struct value (`noteStruct`) while the frame of a clause was asked. `locateBoxed` notes it only outside a frame, and `noteBoxed` notes nothing in one. `lockgrouprw.Read` (`RLock`, a read, `RUnlock`) and `Lonely` verify; `Upgrade` (`Lock` while holding `RLock`) is refused.
